RESEARCH AGENT: END-TO-END OVERHAUL FOR THE DOWNSTREAM ENGINES (prompt 11 v3)

OWNER APPROVAL (D-154): this prompt is the owner's explicit yes for a complete overhaul of the Research agent. Take your checkpoint and tag, cut a new branch from it (feat/research-depth or your choice) and work freely. Nothing in agents/research, apps/research-console, its data files, call prompts, tests, docs or schema is off limits, except the guarantees in section 3 and the standing rules in section 10. Where you see a better way than anything below, do it and say why. I am the planning chat (architect); you own the code. The result is the main thing; cost still counts (section 7).

0. FIRST
- If this is a different machine from the one where the runs were made, restore the raw run folders and the spend record from the archive in the chats repo (projects/mobile-design-engine/notes/research-runtime-2026-11-11/, its README says how; the owner has it); do not overwrite a newer spend-record.json, merge the 7 entries.
- Read: CLAUDE.md, GLOBAL_RULES.md, docs/implementation/CURRENT_STATE.md, DECISIONS.md (D-125 to D-155), your own docs/research-agent/*.md, agents/research README. My design docs (architecture/13 Research design and 14 proof plan) and decision notes (notes/research-*.md, AD-61 to AD-64) are in the chats repo if you can reach them. Everything you need is repeated below.

1. WHO USES THE OUTPUT, AND WHY THAT DECIDES EVERYTHING
Pipeline: Compass (interview, approved Brief) -> Research -> Spec (lanes: Blueprint, Integrations, Design, Screens, Mock, Verify; writes APP_SPEC) -> the front-end engine, which writes the code and builds the app (Expo / React Native; confirm from the repo docs). No person reads Research output. Spec reads it, and through Spec an engine that writes code reads it. A finding is worth something only if Spec can turn it into a decision and the front-end engine can build from it without guessing. So: build-grade facts (exact, typed, sourced, tied to a Brief id), in depth, correct, not hallucinated. Vague prose is worth little. The use rule (doc 13 R21 to R28): Spec may default only on official or corroborated facts; single-source facts are suggestions; unverified facts are never used; numbers and legal facts go on the owner's confirm list; a thin lane is written as "assumed". Every unusable or single-source fact is wasted spend, and every missing integration detail becomes a guess in generated code.

2. WHAT WE MEASURED (Small yoga Bengaluru, 7 live runs on 2026-11-11; Medium and Big have NEVER run with the new code)
- Your yield fixes worked: required slots 2 of 11 -> 6 to 8 of 10; facts 42 -> 93 to 113; cost USD 0.25 -> 0.29 to 0.32; cost per verified fact 0.6 cents -> 0.25 to 0.28 cents.
- Usable depth did not move: official facts stay at 16 (14 to 17%), corroborated 0, unusable (unverified) 28 to 46 facts = 30 to 41%. Main cause: the checker says "partial" on 24 to 37 facts per run; 8 (run F), 18 (C) and 14 (D) of the unverified facts come from official or help-class sources, so they would be official if the claim matched its quote.
- The Integrations lane is filled on paper: Tavily found Google, Zoho, Cybersource, Visa, Dodo for payments in India (not the local leaders); Serper surfaced Razorpay, Paytm, PhonePe, Instamojo but read comparison articles (geeksforgeeks, paritydeals), all unverified. No run gave Spec a payments fact it may default on. Screens lane: 54 to 65 patterns but its required slot is empty (0 of 1); Mock lane 3 to 6 items.
- No query asks for official pages: of 205 recorded queries 184 are plain descriptive, 12 quoted-entity, 9 restricted to a found entity's own domain. data/authority/ only ranks pages after the search.
- Writer and checker are the same model (gpt-6-luna). The planted-error test (python -m mde_research_agent.eval planted) and the consumer probe (eval probe) are built and have never been run.
- Sizes: main pack limit 40,000 tokens; Small stores 23k to 37k (run C 37,284 with 3 appendix items); predicted Medium 36k to 59k, Big 58k to 96k. The owner's confirm list had 35 to 64 entries on Small.
- Do NOT spend time cutting query variants: 67 of 102 verified facts (run F) rest on a page only one query of the topic found.
- Prices: Tavily 8,000 micro-USD a search, Serper 1,000; model calls were 9 to 14% of a Tavily run. Ceilings: Small 32 searches, 64 pages, 330 s; Medium 64, 96, 600 s; Big 120, 210, 960 s. Round 1 issues about 2.2 queries a topic.

3. GUARANTEES TO KEEP (how you keep them is yours)
- Every fact traceable to an exact quote verified by code; every number in a fact inside its quote; every fact checked, official sources included, by a checker independent of the writer wherever possible; no silent deletion (main store plus appendix); honest status (complete only with zero validation errors, partial says why); the verification outcome visible per fact.
- Web content is untrusted: no tools on calls that read it, no URL, action or tool fields in outputs.
- The client's own name and private facts never leave in a query to a search provider (today 0 of 205 queries contain it, but nothing enforces it: add a code guard and a test).
- You may replace or strengthen any mechanism (window size, two-pass extraction, checker routing) if the planted-error test shows the catch rate does not drop.

4. WHAT "BETTER" MEANS (targets, not promises: if you miss one, say why and what you tried)
The measure is usable facts per dollar, and above all default-grade facts (official or corroborated) per dollar.
- Small: required slots at least 70% in 3 of 3 runs; unusable under 15%; default-grade at least 25 (16 today); for payments an in-country provider with official-source facts; zero validation errors; honest status.
- Medium and Big: complete, or an honest partial with its cause; required slots at least 70%; no regulation or integration topic trimmed by the topic cap; one provider dossier (section 5D) per integration the Brief names, at least 60% of fields filled from official sources where docs exist (report the actual); a compliance register for every regime the Brief names; main pack ranked; confirm list ranked.
- Checker: planted-error catch at least 90% overall and 80% per kind, false rejection at most 5%; otherwise a second-vendor checker through the gateway (if the gateway has none, report it, do not add a vendor outside the gateway).
- Consumer probe: fewer ASSUMED on researched needs, invented not higher.
- Cost per default-grade fact at most USD 0.013 on Small (0.018 to 0.020 today); total per run near Small 0.32 to 0.40, Medium 0.55 to 0.85, Big 1.0 to 1.8, or explain.

5. WORKSTREAMS (order is yours; my reasoning: free things first, then the things everything else depends on)

A. Intake that survives Compass changes, and the six test inputs
- Today the intake accepts only format 2 with an approved baseline and a consumer_views.research block, and refuses the rest (ExportRefused: not_format_2, not_approved, missing_research_view). Three of the six new inputs would be refused. Build an adapter layer: detect the format version; build the internal ResearchInput from the research view when present, else straight from the Brief (it holds domain_facts, research_requests, search_hints, apps, roles, scope, integrations, glossary, references); tolerate a missing structure, modules, quality block, tier tags or request origin (the flags no_quality_block, request_origin_missing, tiers_read_from_brief already exist); ignore unknown keys; never crash; refuse only when the Brief is unusable, with a plain reason.
- A missing approved baseline or a hash mismatch: accept the test inputs with a visible input_binding "unverified" flag in the preview and the report (production exports always carry the baseline; add no switch).
- Keep all Compass-format knowledge in that one adapter, with one versioned fixture per known format and contract tests. Write docs/research-agent/compass-contract.md: the fields Research needs, their meaning, which are optional and what degrades without them. The owner will change Compass's output later (future scope): a new Compass version should need only an adapter change and a fixture, never pipeline changes. Do not build for imagined versions.
- Copy the six files into docs/compass/research-inputs/ (leave the originals in docs/compass/outputs/ untouched) and extend that README. Preview all nine inputs (three earlier plus six new) at zero cost: size class, signals, trust on or off, planned topics, "not run", "dropped", warnings. For each Brief request, show which topic carries it.

B. Discovery: official first, the right providers, any country
1. Official-first: for rules, regulation, platform rules and provider documentation, one query variant per topic asks for the authority domains in prefer mode (Tavily native; Serper has no prefer mode, so a separate restricted query), replacing a descriptive variant so the number of searches does not change.
2. Entity follow-up: every provider the Brief needs, once discovered, gets its own documentation, pricing and help pages read (domain-restricted). Today only 9 of 205 queries do this. Competitors in a matrix: bounded.
3. Vendor topics (payments, messaging, maps, tracking, identity, accounting): run both engines every time (Serper costs a tenth), fuse as today; Tavily stays primary for page text. Your call on wider ensembles if cheap and measured.
4. Countries without a registry: do not hand-curate the world. (a) Seed registries for the test countries and add the missing India sources; verify every domain is real and official before adding (examples to verify, not to trust: Portugal cnpd.pt, bportugal.pt, portaldasfinancas.gov.pt, eur-lex.europa.eu; Senegal cdp.sn, bceao.int, artp.sn, dgid.sn; Mexico stps.gob.mx, dof.gob.mx, sat.gob.mx; Costa Rica mtss.go.cr, ccss.sa.cr; Guatemala mintrabajo.gob.gt; India indiacode.nic.in, egazette.gov.in, consumeraffairs.gov.in, msamb.com, sfacindia.com, nabard.org, arai.in, dot.gov.in, irdai.gov.in, abdm.gov.in, nmc.org.in, cdsco.gov.in, mohfw.gov.in). (b) Generic official inference from data patterns (gov.*, gob.*, gouv.*, go.*, europa.eu, .int, and a provider's own domain for facts about itself), flagged official_inferred; code decides the class, never the model.
5. Languages: queries in the authority's language for rules (fr, pt, es), English for global vendor docs; claims in English with source-language quotes; the checker and the number check must work across languages, decimal commas, thin and no-break spaces as thousand separators, and currencies (FCFA/XOF, MXN, GTQ, CRC, EUR, INR). Devanagari digits are already handled.
6. Multi-country Briefs (Central America names Mexico, Guatemala, Costa Rica): research regulation and provider availability once per country; share norms, design and screens across countries (do not triple the cost). The place model must carry a list of countries. countries.yaml lacks gt and cr (not in Tavily's enum): fall back to language, TLD and query terms and flag locale_weak.

C. Evidence quality
1. Claim fit: find out why 24 to 37 facts per run are "partial". Tactics: quote-first extraction (pick the supporting passage, write the claim only from it), shorter literal claims, one fact one quote, tighten then re-check. A partial claim climbs only if the same checker then says supported and every number is still in the quote. Never loosen the checker. Check what the 150-word window does to false partials.
2. Independent checker: planted-error test first, then act on the result (section 4). Measure the false-partial rate on the in-app 60-fact spot-check.
3. Corroboration (0 corroborated items in 7 runs; norms with 3 or more sources 0 to 4% against doc 14's 70% row): first the free check doc 14 names (is norm-key merging under-merging the same fact; are enough competitors read), then a bounded second-source search for high-impact facts in required slots, restricted to independent domains.
4. Find out why the Screens required slot stays empty with 54 to 65 patterns and why Mock is thin; fix both.
5. Recency and conflicts: capture effective dates for rules, prefer the newest official version, flag superseded rules, keep both values when sources conflict.

D. Build-grade content (what Spec and the front-end engine actually need)
1. Provider dossier for every integration the Brief names: identity and official docs URL; countries served (including the Brief's); products; auth method; endpoints, events, webhooks; sandbox; SDKs and React Native or Expo support (config plugin or native module, Expo Go versus development build); fees (typed); limits; onboarding and KYC requirements and lead time; settlement timing; compliance (PCI, licence); status and deprecation. Each field has its own quote and evidence label; an unknown field is an explicit null with a reason; completeness is computed by code. A front-end engineer should be able to write the integration from the dossier alone.
2. Typed values: fees, limits, durations, percentages as {amount, currency or unit, period, condition} beside the quote span, verified by code.
3. Platform and build constraints (new job; official sources developer.apple.com, developer.android.com, docs.expo.dev, reactnative.dev are already in global.yaml) for each capability the Brief implies: payments for physical services versus digital goods under store rules, push notifications, background location (fleet drivers), camera and photo upload, offline sync, biometric sign-in, Bluetooth and devices, health data policies, children's data, deep links, map SDKs. Output: constraint, platform, rule, source, effect on the build (needs a development build, a permission, a review risk). Small cost, large value: it stops Spec from specifying what cannot ship.
4. Compliance register per country and regime: statement, authority, effective date, which flows, roles or data it applies to, penalty if stated, source; legal-adjacent, never advice; all on the confirm list.
5. Flows with exceptions (steps, alternates, exceptions, empty, error and offline states), screen patterns (sections, states, validations, microcopy), entity patterns and lifecycles (only what is stated), local formats (phone, address, currency, ID, date), sample-data hints by locale, design conventions including script and RTL needs, competitor matrix with features and pricing model.
6. Brief linkage: every item names the Brief ids it applies to (feature, role, entity, flow, integration, module) or NEW. For each Spec lane, list the decisions it must make and check that Research gave something for each, or an explicit gap.

Big: add playbooks per module cluster (at most 8), a dossier per external system, role workflow exceptions, a compliance register per regime and country, realistic data by entity, trends.
- Planning: the plan is built by code and trimmed to a topic cap (Small 10, Medium 16, Big 26). Big's Brief has 20 requests, 11 modules, 22 entities, 74 facts (Small: 5, 4, 5, 14); 11 of Big's 20 requests are rules or official-portal integrations (GST, e-way bill, e-invoice, FASTag, driver and vehicle documents, data protection, SMS rules, payments, GPS). No regulation or integration topic may be trimmed before lower-value topics; raise caps from data (cost is linear: about 0.008 a search, 0.0005 a page); rounds continue while required slots are empty and gains continue; time limits must not end a run that is still finding facts (Medium 600 s and Big 960 s are likely too tight: measure and raise).

G. Robustness and edge cases (decide each, record it)
- Pages: PDF (text layer, up to 60 pages), JavaScript-only portals, paywalls, login and cookie walls, robots: spares and refunds exist; use the provider's own page text as a fallback; scanned PDFs report no_content honestly.
- Provider outage, 429, quota: partial with the reason; the run can be resumed.

- Thin or filler research requests: rebuild topics from the Brief (all 7 runs did: 1 trusted and 4 template of 5 requests); say so in the report.
- Duplicate facts across topics: merge by normalised key. Contradictory sources: keep both, flag.

  P4 is done and committed on branch `worktree-agent-a87a2906477cab0e8`, head `c2183a7`, three commits on top of `c86d4dd`. Nothing was pushed. All items 1 to 7 are implemented. Nothing ran live or called a search provider; the only network use was WebFetch/WebSearch to verify domains.

  3. `c2183a7` docs: new `docs/research-agent/discovery-and-planning.md`, the index line in `docs/README.md`, preview fields in `docs/api/research-agent.md`, a pointer on the package page.
  
  **Planner: topics run / not run per input (preview, all nine inputs)**
  
  | Input | Size | Before | After | Risk topics in "after" |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | delhi | Small | 10 / 22 | 17 / 15 | 6 |

  | porto | Small | 10 / 33 | 17 / 26 | 6 |

  Decision for you: Medium now runs 34 to 38 topics and Big 54 to 69, because risk topics sit outside the 12/24/38 cap as you asked. That is roughly 75 to 84 searches for Medium (hard 96) and 119 to 152 for Big (hard 170). To shrink it, change `soft.targets` or `risk_topics` in `ceilings.yaml`, one line each.
  
  **What changed, by item**
  - **Planner:**
    - Risk topics are kept outside the cap, and `risk_topics` (5/18/36) is only a safety valve.
    - A job keeps its best topic only if that topic has a required slot, and entities the Brief names get a +12 priority bonus.
    - Ceilings are set per D-163; round-one pages are shared between topics, and soft-to-hard time extension is in `Director._time_stop`.
    - A named reference with no public footprint ends `not_found` and leaves the required-slot gate.
    - Per-country topics via `scope: country_each` or `per_country: true`; the best 3 candidates are expanded per country.
    - The preview lists each not-run topic with `est_searches` and `est_usd_micros`.
  - **Official-first:** an `official` variant replaces one descriptive variant. Tavily prefers the registry domains; Serper gets `site:` restricted queries only on risk topics. `rsch.write_queries` is now v2, and I added fr, pt and es intent words.
  - **Own-site follow-up:** after round one, documentation, pricing and help queries restricted to each discovered provider's domain, capped 3/5/8 and counted in the budgets.
  - **Both engines:** done in the worker via `ctx.second_search` and `fuse_engines`. One search counts against the ceiling and both providers' cost is booked. Second-engine cassettes go to `cassettes/second`.
  - **Registries:** seeded `pt`, `sn`, `mx`, `gt`, `cr`, `eu` and extended `in`, each entry with `fetch`, `verified_on` and a per-country `language`.
    - The pattern rules `gov.*`, `gob.*`, `gouv.*`, `go.*`, `*.gov`, `europa.eu`, `.int` and a provider's own domain give `official_inferred`.
    - A `gov.xx` host does not count as official for another country's topic.
  - **Numbers:** decimal comma, dot thousands, thin/no-break/narrow/plain-space thousands (`2024 500` stays two numbers), currency tokens (`$350` is no longer backed by `₹350`), typed `amount`/`currency`/`period`/`unit` in the numeric fields, and French/Spanish/Portuguese number words on the quote side.
  
  **Domains**
  - **Verified and added** (WebFetch and/or a web search restricted to the domain, 2026-11-13):
    - Portugal: `cnpd.pt`, `bportugal.pt`, `portaldasfinancas.gov.pt`, `eur-lex.europa.eu`.
    - Senegal: `cdp.sn`, `bceao.int`, `artp.sn`, `dgid.sn`.
    - Mexico: `stps.gob.mx`, `dof.gob.mx`, `sat.gob.mx`.
    - Costa Rica: `mtss.go.cr`, `ccss.sa.cr`.
    - Guatemala: `mintrabajo.gob.gt`.
    - India: `indiacode.nic.in`, `egazette.gov.in`, `consumeraffairs.gov.in`, `msamb.com`, `sfacindia.com`, `nabard.org`, `dot.gov.in`, `irdai.gov.in`, `abdm.gov.in`, `nmc.org.in`, `cdsco.gov.in`, `mohfw.gov.in`.
  - **Extras I also verified and added:** `diariodarepublica.pt`, `banxico.org.mx`, `imss.gob.mx`, `sat.gob.gt`, `sib.gob.gt`, `bccr.fi.cr`, `prodhab.go.cr`, `araiindia.com`.
  - **Rejected:** `arai.in`. It gave a TLS error and a search says the body publishes at `araiindia.com`, which is listed instead.
  - **cdp.sn:** its home page is an empty "CDP" shell, but a search restricted to the domain returned its own pages (law, rights, decrees). I recorded it as `fetch: blocked`.
  - **`fetch` values:** `ok` for cnpd.pt, portaldasfinancas.gov.pt, eur-lex, bceao.int, dgid.sn, mtss.go.cr, mintrabajo.gob.gt, banxico, bccr and most of India. `blocked` for bportugal.pt, diariodarepublica.pt, cdp.sn, artp.sn, stps.gob.mx, imss.gob.mx, sat.gob.gt, sib.gob.gt, ccss.sa.cr, prodhab.go.cr, indiacode.nic.in, egazette.gov.in, dot.gov.in, mohfw.gov.in, araiindia.com. `unresolved` for `dof.gob.mx` and `sat.gob.mx`; pages of both are in the search index. The fetch tool here may differ from our product fetcher, so treat these as hints.
  
  **Decisions to log**
  - `risk_topics` valve values and the derivation in the `ceilings.yaml` comment; a rule topic is a risk topic only with compliance-fact or request evidence.
  - Per-country expansion is capped at `per_country_max: 3` candidates and never spreads a request-carrying topic.
  - `official_inferred` counts as official in the evidence ladder and confidence, and shares rank 0 in `CLASS_RANK`, so `confidence_of` thresholds are unchanged. A currency swap is now caught by code, so the planted-error `currency_swapped` kind no longer measures the checker alone.
  - Soft pages in `ceilings.yaml` are 44/90/170, my choice since D-163 did not give them.
  
  **Open issues**
  - Replay (`live_runs.py`) does not read `cassettes/second/`, so a replay of a both-engine run lacks the second list.
  - The mock-web tests had to raise hard search ceilings (checker, page-budget, rs30 tests) and the store-token limit (test_store), because larger plans exhaust the mock ceiling.
  - The authority files are seed lists.
  
  **Requested changes in files I do not own**
  - `schemas.py` and the `contracts/research.v5.schema.json` I regenerated: `SourceClass` gained `official_inferred`; re-run `uv run python -m mde_research_agent.schemas --write` after P5 merges.
  - `assemble/items.py` (ladder and `CLASS_RANK`), `review.py`, `service.py` (`second_search`), `compose.py`, `engine/verify.py`, `engine/factory.py`, `domain/plan.py`, `domain/research.py` and `tests/support/testing.py` got small edits that the lead and P5 should expect when merging.
  - Services, data, trust and local job YAML: I touched services, data and trust only, adding `risk`, `risk_from_candidate`, `official_first`, `engines` and `scope`/`per_country`. `features.yaml` is untouched.
  - P5's extraction prompt should keep claims in English with source-language quotes.
  - `estimates.yaml` still has the old topic counts; refresh it from D-163 or measured runs.

# Research agent overhaul: report (2026-11-13)

Branch `feat/research-depth` (pushed), tag `research/depth-20261113`.
Offline tests: 2,245 passed. Full notes: docs/research-agent/depth-overhaul-as-built-2026-11-13.md

## Where things stand
- All code and docs are done and pushed.
- Live proof is partly done. The OpenAI account has no credits (HTTP 429 "You have no credits remaining"). Three Medium
  attempts failed on it. Until credits are added, these cannot run: Medium on the final code, the consumer probe, Porto,
  Big on Tavily.
- Money: USD 4.68 of the 5.50 total is booked (the repo ceiling is 8.00 and the shared ledger already held 2.37).
- Search credits: Tavily itself reports 320 of 1,000 used (the app's own counter said 323); Serper has about 1,560 left
  (counted by the app, because Serper has no balance endpoint). Both show in the console (a bar chip and a Budget panel).
  The panel warns only; it never blocks a run.

## What was wrong
- 30 to 41% of facts were unusable because the checker said "partial" (82% of workflows, 75% of provider facts).
- 94% of those partials already had the unsupported fields removed, but stayed "partial".
- The Screens slot could never fill (a code bug).
- The planner dropped important topics (payments, regulation) for empty ones. Medium and Big left 41 and 39 planned topics
  unrun, so they were no deeper than Small.
- No query ever asked for official pages; payments in India came back as Visa and PayPal.
- Page fetches timed out wholesale at large size.

## What was built
- Intake that survives Compass changes: any format version, a missing approval record is a visible flag, not a refusal.
  All nine inputs load. One adapter, one fixture per format, and a contract note.
- Query privacy guard: the client's name and private facts cannot reach a search provider (code, plus a test over all inputs).
- Global search-credit tracker (Tavily read from its own usage endpoint, Serper counted) and its console panel.
- Evidence quality: checker v3, a tighten-and-ask-once-more step for partial facts, the Screens fix, the planted-error
  test, and a re-extraction experiment.
- Planner and discovery: risk topics sit outside the cap, official-first queries, own-site follow-up for the most important
  providers, a discovery query on vendor topics, Serper as a second engine, registries for Portugal, Senegal, Mexico,
  Guatemala, Costa Rica, the EU and more of India (every domain checked on the web), and numbers across languages and
  currencies.
- Build-grade content for Spec and the app engine: provider dossiers with typed fees and limits (each field has its own
  quote and an evidence label, or an explicit null with a reason), platform and store constraints, a compliance register, a
  ranked confirm list, lane decisions, and a reference `pack(need_card)` for Spec.
- Robustness: fetch retries, bounded text extraction, longer timeouts at scale, failover cool-down, and a model call that
  waits out an open circuit. The main pack is now bounded.

## Is the checker honest?
Planted-error test on real facts: 98.9% caught, 0% falsely rejected, and 0 of 31 planted errors got through the new
tighten step. On re-extracted facts, items with no supported evidence fell from about 26% to 14.6%.

## Live runs on the new code
| Run | Status | Searches / pages | USD | Required slots | Facts: official / single / unverified |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Small yoga, Tavily, run 1 | partial (search ceiling) | 40 / 60 | 0.39 | 12 of 19 | 73 / 18 / 15 |
| Small yoga, Tavily, run 2 (after fixes) | partial (search ceiling) | 52 / 80 | 0.49 | 15 of 19 (79%) | 103 / 31 / 16 (11%) |
| Small yoga, Serper | partial | 52 / 79 | 0.13 | 11 of 19 (58%) | 89 / 78 / 23 |
| Medium Nashik, Tavily (before ceilings were raised) | partial (page ceiling) | 91 / 130 | 0.86 | 20 of 40 (50%) | 185 / 39 / 32 (12.5%) |
| Big freight, Serper (after the fetch fix) | partial (page ceiling) | 199 / 300 | 0.52 | 55 of 67 (82%) | 307 / 205 / 61 (11%) |
| Medium Nashik, Serper, three attempts | failed (OpenAI credits) | about 68 each | 0.11 each | none | none |

## Against the targets
- Met: unusable facts under 15% (Small 11%, Big 11%); Small default-grade facts at least 25 (103); an in-country payments
  provider with official facts on Small (Cashfree, PhonePe, 91 to 92% of dossier fields from their own pages); cost per
  default-grade fact at most 0.013 (Small 0.0048, Big 0.0017); planted-error catch rates; no risk topic trimmed by the cap.
- Not met: Small required slots at 70% in 3 of 3 runs (one run at 79%, the first at 63%); Small cost per run (0.49 against
  0.32 to 0.40); Medium slots (50%, before the ceilings were raised); compliance register per regime (thin: 1 to 3
  entries); consumer probe (not run).
- Caveat: "official" includes facts a provider states on its own site (`official_inferred`). That is what Spec needs for
  integrations, but it is why the count is high. Please spot-check run `rrn_01M796H29N35AX60V2R0D5RN3S` (60 facts in the app).

## Provider choice
Tavily stays the default. Serper filled fewer Small slots (11 of 19 against 15) at about a quarter of the cost, so by your
rule it stays the failover and the second engine. I used Serper for Big only because Tavily's estimate did not fit the
remaining money.

## Not proven or not built
- Medium on the final code, Big on Tavily, the consumer probe, Porto: never run (credits).
- The store-limit, label, validator and circuit-wait fixes were made after the last successful run; tested offline only.
- Not built on purpose: resuming a run, a second-source check for corroboration, quote-first extraction, and a second-vendor

- The console's new views were not opened in a browser (playwright is not installed here).
- Porto, Dakar, Pune, hospital, Delhi and Central America were previewed at zero cost but not run live.

## To finish (after adding OpenAI credits)
About USD 1.7 in total: Medium on Tavily about 1.1 (or about 0.4 on Serper), the consumer probe 0.15, Porto 0.45.
The first two fit under the 5.50 total, but all three together do not (4.68 is already booked), so I'd raise the total
or drop Porto. Start the app with `RESEARCH_LIVE_BUDGET_USD=5.50 RESEARCH_TRIPWIRE_USD=5.00 uv run python
scripts/research_app.py start`.

### CLAUDE

**Short answer: yes, it can work, but it is not a key swap.** The gateway has only an OpenAI provider, so we would build a Claude one first. That is about a day or two of work, and none of it needs a live call until the tests are ready. Which parts I confirmed and which I didn't is below.

**What I confirmed**
- **Price is the same as now.** Haiku 5.5 is $0.10 in and $0.50 out per million tokens, identical to `gpt-6-luna`, per [Anthropic's pricing page](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing). Prompts over 100,000 tokens cost 5 times more, but our largest prompts are about 13,000 tokens.
- **Haiku 5.5 supports structured JSON output** (`claude-haiku-5-5`), per [Anthropic's structured-outputs docs](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs). The parameter is `output_config.format`.
- **The code only knows OpenAI.** The gateway builds exactly one live provider, `OpenAIResponsesProvider`. The price list has one model, and an unlisted model is refused. The strict-schema converter is written for OpenAI's rules.

**Where it would break without work**
1. **No Claude provider exists.** We'd add one inside the gateway, about the size of the OpenAI one (roughly 300 lines), plus a `claude-haiku-5-5` price entry and a rule choosing the vendor by model name. That keeps Compass untouched.
2. **Schemas differ.**
   - Anthropic rejects `minimum`, `maximum` and `minLength`/`maxLength`, and allows `minItems` of only 0 or 1. Our converter keeps some of these, which OpenAI accepts, so it needs a Claude variant.
   - Our extraction schemas are large. I could not find a documented size limit, so a "schema too complex" error is the biggest unknown until we try. The first use of each schema is also slower.
3. **Project rules.** `.importlinter` and the gateway docs say only the gateway may import `openai`. Anthropic needs the same rule added. Rule 13 is about gating live mode, so a second vendor's key doesn't break it, but it's your rule, so you decide.
4. **Smaller mappings:** error and rate-limit handling, usage and cache fields, and how "low effort" reasoning maps to Claude. Your Anthropic account's rate limits also matter: Research runs up to 16 parallel model calls.

**What I can't promise: quality.** All prompts were tuned on `gpt-6-luna`. Claude may quote differently or fill fields differently. We already have the right tests for this: the planted-error test (about 1 cent) and the re-extraction experiment (about 10 cents), both runnable on Claude before any full run.

**One real advantage.** The checker is currently the same model as the writer, which was the weakness I flagged earlier. With Claude as the checker and OpenAI as the writer, we'd have a true second vendor.

**Suggested order**
1. Build the provider and the schema variant.
2. Put Claude on the checker only, then run the planted-error test.
3. Measure Claude as the writer with the re-extraction test.
4. Decide from the numbers.

Add your Anthropic key to `.env` yourself as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; I never read that file. Also, adding OpenAI credits would unblock the remaining Medium, probe and Porto runs sooner than this build. Say the word and I'll start on the provider.

# Building LLM-Powered Applications with Claude

This skill helps you build LLM-powered applications with Claude. Choose the right surface based on your needs, detect the project language, then read the relevant language-specific documentation.

## Before You Start

Scan the target file (or, if no target file, the prompt and project) for non-Anthropic provider markers - `import openai`, `from openai`, `langchain_openai`, `OpenAI(`, `gpt-4`, `gpt-5`, file names like `agent-openai.py` or `*-generic.py`, or any explicit instruction to keep the code provider-neutral. If you find any, stop and tell the user that this skill produces Claude/Anthropic SDK code; ask whether they want to switch the file to Claude or want a non-Claude implementation. Do not edit a non-Anthropic file with Anthropic SDK calls. (Exception: the `prompt-audit` subcommand is non-interactive and does not stop here - it records non-Anthropic provider markers in its report's stated assumptions and never proposes switching a non-Anthropic file to the Anthropic SDK.)

## Output Requirement

When the user asks you to add, modify, or implement a Claude feature, your code must call Claude through one of:

1. **The official Anthropic SDK** for the project's language (`anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `com.anthropic.*`, etc.). This is the default whenever a supported SDK exists for the project.
2. **Raw HTTP** (`curl`, `requests`, `fetch`, `httpx`, etc.) - only when the user explicitly asks for cURL/REST/raw HTTP, the project is a shell/cURL project, or the language has no official SDK.

Never mix the two - don't reach for `requests`/`fetch` in a Python or TypeScript project just because it feels lighter. Never fall back to OpenAI-compatible shims.

**Never guess SDK usage.** Function names, class names, namespaces, method signatures, and import paths must come from explicit documentation - either the `{lang}/` files in this skill or the official SDK repositories or documentation links listed in `shared/live-sources.md`. If the binding you need is not explicitly documented in the skill files, fetch the relevant SDK repo from `shared/live-sources.md` before writing code. Do not infer Ruby/Java/Go/PHP/C# APIs from cURL shapes or from another language's SDK.

If you cannot call WebFetch yet and ToolSearch is among your tools, call ToolSearch with `select:WebFetch` first; of the tools it loads, call only the one named exactly `WebFetch`.

**If fetching or repository access fails** (network restricted, timeouts, clone blocked): do not keep retrying - write code from the patterns and namespace/package tables in the `{lang}/` file, run the compiler or interpreter on it, and iterate on the error output. For statically-typed SDKs (C#, Java, Go) a compile-fix loop against local errors reaches working code faster than blocked network research.

## Defaults

Unless the user requests otherwise:

For the Claude model version, please use Claude Opus 5.5, which you can access via the exact model string `claude-opus-5-5`. Please default to using adaptive thinking (`thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`) for anything remotely complicated. And finally, please default to streaming for any request that may involve long input, long output, or high `max_tokens` - it prevents hitting request timeouts. Use the SDK's `.get_final_message()` / `.finalMessage()` helper to get the complete response if you don't need to handle individual stream events. When a streaming request defines user-defined (client) tools, set `eager_input_streaming: true` on each of those tools so large tool inputs (file contents, code, documents) stream as they are generated instead of arriving in one burst after the server finishes buffering them; the client then owns validation: the SDKs' tolerant parsers can return a silently truncated input instead of raising, so validate each parsed tool input against its schema before running it (the typed runner helpers such as `betaZodTool` / typed `@beta_tool` do this; `betaTool()` JSON-Schema tools and manual loops must validate themselves), treat a failure like invalid JSON (`INVALID_JSON` error `tool_result` when you hold the block, re-issue otherwise), check `max_tokens` / `refusal` stop reasons before running tools, and catch only the SDK's JSON error, never its typed API errors - pattern in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` -> Eager input streaming. Leave it off for non-streaming requests, for server tools, and when the request goes through a proxy or an older Bedrock model deployment that rejects the field.

## Warning: API Drift - Your Training Prior May Be Stale

Several common Claude API shapes changed in 2025-2026. If you recall a pattern from training, verify it against the `{lang}/` files in this skill before writing - the rows below are the most frequent drift points:

| Area | Stale prior | Current API |
|---|---|---|
| Extended thinking | `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens=[REDACTED] | On Claude 4.6+ models: `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`. `budget_tokens` is deprecated on Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 and **rejected with a 400** on Fable 5/5.1/5.2 / Sonnet 5.5 / Sonnet 5 / Opus 5.5 / 5 / 4.8 / 4.7. Pre-4.6 models still use `budget_tokens`. |
| Web search / web fetch tool type | `web_search_20250305`, `web_fetch_20250910` | `web_search_20260209`, `web_fetch_20260209` (dynamic filtering) on Fable and Mythos models, Opus 5.5/5/4.8/4.7/4.6, Sonnet 5.5, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6. Older models keep the basic variants; on Vertex AI only basic `web_search_20250305` is available (web fetch is not on Vertex) - see the Server Tools QR below. |
| PHP parameter names | snake_case wire names as named args (`max_tokens`) | Top-level named args are camelCase (`maxTokens`). Nested array keys vary by feature (e.g. `'taskBudget'`, `'skillID'`, `'mcp_server_name'`) - copy the exact key from the documented example; do not bulk-convert. |
| Managed Agents credentials | Keep secrets host-side via custom tools (the only option before vaults shipped) | Vault `environment_variable` credentials - stored by Anthropic, substituted at egress, so the sandbox holds only a placeholder unless a service sends the secret back (`shared/managed-agents-tools.md` -> Vaults). Host-side custom tools remain the fallback for self-hosted sandboxes. |
| Files API / Skills | `client.beta.files.*` / `client.beta.skills.*` with beta `files-api-2025-04-14` / `skills-2025-10-02` | Out of beta: `client.files.*` / `client.skills.*`, no beta header. In current SDKs `client.beta.files` / `client.beta.skills` have breaking shape changes from previous versions, matching the stable namespaces - migrate per `shared/live-sources.md` -> Files API / Skills Guide. |

## Subcommands

If the User Request at the bottom of this prompt is a bare subcommand string (no prose), search every **Subcommands** table in this document - including any in sections appended below - and follow the matching Action column directly. This lets users invoke specific flows via `/claude-api <subcommand>`. If no table in the document matches, treat the request as normal prose.

| Subcommand | Action |
|---|---|
| `migrate` | Migrate existing Claude API code to a newer model. **Read `shared/model-migration.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (confirm scope - ask which files/directories before any edit), Step 1 (classify each file), then the per-target breaking-changes section. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. If the user did not name a target model, ask which model to migrate to in the same turn as the scope question. After the per-target changes are applied, audit the in-scope prompt text, tool descriptions, and request code against `shared/prompt-audit.md` - prompting written for the source model is part of every migration, and it does not announce itself. |
| `prompt-audit` | Audit existing prompts, tool descriptions, skills, and agent configuration files (`CLAUDE.md`, rule files, commands, subagents) for dated patterns ("cruft"): text written for older models, and instructions the repository has outgrown or that contradict each other. **Read `shared/prompt-audit.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (establish scope and target model from the request and the repository - state the assumptions in the report, do not stop to ask), inventory, provenance, then the pattern scan. Produce both deliverables in full - the audit report (findings with `file:line`, pattern, why it's obsolete, confidence) and a proposed diff - without pausing for confirmation; apply edits only if the request explicitly asked for them. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. |
| `upgrade` | Upgrade the project's Anthropic SDK dependency across a major version. Trailing words may name the language and/or a scope (`upgrade python`, `upgrade python sdk src/`). Read the declared version first. Every SDK, Python 0.x included, and the `ant` CLI: follow the `api-version` row if the upgrade reaches or passes the release in its guide's "Set the version" table (an upgrade to the newest release does); if not, point the user at that SDK's CHANGELOG (repositories in `shared/live-sources.md`), except Python 0.x to 1.x, below. Don't improvise from the Python guide. Python 0.x to 1.x (the user's choice, or where `shared/api-version-migration.md` sends you): **read `python/claude-api/sdk-upgrade.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (confirm scope, then establish the current and target versions - a published 1.x must exist before you write a pin), the Step 1 inventory, each numbered section, then verification and the report. Do not summarize the guide - execute it. This is not model migration - to move code to a newer Claude model, use `migrate`. |
| `api-version` | Move code to a newer API version (`anthropic-version` header). **Read `shared/api-version-migration.md` immediately** and follow it in order. |
| `cost-optimize` | Reduce what existing Claude API code costs to run, without sacrificing output quality. **Read `shared/cost-optimization.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (establish scope, quality bar, and baseline), the token profile - measured through the Usage and Cost Admin API when the user has an Admin API key, from the app's own `response.usage` logs when it has those (ask), or estimated from the code otherwise - then a savings-ranked shortlist of levers (quoted in dollars, % of bill, or relative buckets depending on which of those data sources you have), free wins (caching, input-token hygiene, loop hygiene, output-token hygiene, batch) before tradeoffs (budgets, effort, model choice, multi-model); any lever that earns a place becomes its own diff - proposed by default, applied and measured against the eval covering the traffic it touches when the user asks and approves - and "no changes recommended" is a valid outcome. Two standing rules: every run that exercises the model spends real money, so get the user's approval first; and when context for a lever is missing, work through it interactively with the user - this workflow is not expected to one-shot the audit. Do not summarize the guide - execute it; presenting the profile and the ranked plan to the user is part of executing it. |
| `latency-optimize` | Reduce how long existing Claude API code makes people wait, without giving up output quality. **Read `shared/latency-optimization.md` immediately.** Words after the subcommand are the user's request: start from them and from what the repository shows - answer a question, try the change they named, or find where the time goes and propose a few changes. The guide says when to open its reference files and `shared/latency-optimization/latency_probe.py`. No change counts as a win without the eval, nothing billed runs without the user's yes, and "no changes recommended" is a valid outcome. |
| `build-eval` | Help the user build an eval set for their Claude-powered app. **Read `shared/evals/build-eval.md` immediately** and run its interview: Step 0 (what's being evaluated), Step 1 (source the prompts - existing eval / transcripts / synthesized), Step 2 (grading method), Step 3 (runnable script + measured cost). Get the user's explicit sign-off on the inputs, the grading method, and the cost before producing the eval. |
| `preserved-thinking-migration` | Make an existing integration compatible with preserved thinking - the check that keeps a thinking block valid only in the conversation that produced it. **Read `shared/preserved-thinking-migration.md` immediately** and follow it in order: Step 0 (scope, traffic classes, platform and model, enforcement status, quality bar, baseline), Step 0.5 (prove the check is running with the three-request self-test), Step 1 (capture request bodies, diff consecutive pairs with `shared/preserved-thinking-migration/prefix_diff.py`, scan the code for the causes, name each edit and whether it is deliberate), Step 2 (replay a test slice with `prefix_mismatch_behavior: "drop_block"` under the `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01` header, count new dropped blocks per conversation, read the diagnosis header when present), Step 3 (one cause per diff in order of reasoning lost - proposed by default, applied when the user asks - then re-measure, keep or revert; the three-arm protocol when an eval exists), the model-switch section (in `shared/preserved-thinking-migration/causes.md`, with the cause table and the keep list) when the harness routes between models, Step 4 (the break profile and the changes). Two standing rules: every replay spends real money, so get the user's approval for the measurement budget first; and "no changes recommended" - the slice replayed thinking and nothing was dropped - is a valid outcome. Causes that have an append-only form only under a newer beta (keep-tail and background compaction: `compact-2026-09-04`; same-name tool changes: `inline-tools-2026-09-15`) are, where that beta is not available, measured and decided, not rewritten. For the *why* (the three-step check, the append-only edit table) it chains to `shared/model-migration.md` -> Breaking change 3; do not summarize the guide - execute it. |
| `hillclimb` | Iteratively improve the user's app against an existing eval. **Read `shared/evals/eval-hillclimb.md` immediately** and follow it: Step 0 (confirm a runnable eval exists - if not, route to `build-eval`), Step 1 (what to change / what's off-limits), Step 2 (budget + stopping condition from measured per-run cost), get the plan approved, then the read->propose->apply->run->record loop with on-disk state and a train/validation/test split. |

## Language Detection

Before reading code examples, determine which language the user is working in (exception: for the `prompt-audit` subcommand, skip this section's ask steps - the audit is non-interactive and its inventory is language-agnostic; when no language is inferable, proceed without asking and state the assumption in the report):

1. **Look at project files** to infer the language:

 - `*.py`, `requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `Pipfile` -> **Python** - read from `python/`
 - `*.ts`, `*.tsx`, `package.json`, `tsconfig.json` -> **TypeScript** - read from `typescript/`
 - `*.js`, `*.jsx` (no `.ts` files present) -> **TypeScript** - JS uses the same SDK, read from `typescript/`
 - `*.java`, `pom.xml`, `build.gradle` -> **Java** - read from `java/`
 - `*.kt`, `*.kts`, `build.gradle.kts` -> **Java** - Kotlin uses the Java SDK, read from `java/`
 - `*.scala`, `build.sbt` -> **Java** - Scala uses the Java SDK, read from `java/`
 - `*.go`, `go.mod` -> **Go** - read from `go/`
 - `*.rb`, `Gemfile` -> **Ruby** - read from `ruby/`
 - `*.cs`, `*.csproj` -> **C#** - read from `csharp/`
 - `*.php`, `composer.json` -> **PHP** - read from `php/`

2. **If multiple languages detected** (e.g., both Python and TypeScript files):

 - Check which language the user's current file or question relates to
 - If still ambiguous, ask: "I detected both Python and TypeScript files. Which language are you using for the Claude API integration?"

3. **If language can't be inferred** (empty project, no source files, or unsupported language):

 - Use AskUserQuestion with options: Python, TypeScript, Java, Go, Ruby, cURL/raw HTTP, C#, PHP
 - If AskUserQuestion is unavailable, default to Python examples and note: "Showing Python examples. Let me know if you need a different language."

4. **If unsupported language detected** (Rust, Swift, C++, Elixir, etc.):

 - Suggest cURL/raw HTTP examples from `curl/` and note that community SDKs may exist
 - Offer to show Python or TypeScript examples as reference implementations

5. **If user needs cURL/raw HTTP examples**, read from `curl/`.

### Language-Specific Feature Support

Every SDK language above supports both the beta Tool Runner and Managed Agents (beta) - Python (`@beta_tool` decorator), TypeScript (`betaZodTool` + Zod), Java (annotated classes), Go (`BetaToolRunner` in the `toolrunner` pkg), Ruby (`BaseTool` + `tool_runner`), C# (`BetaToolRunner` + raw JSON schema), PHP (`BetaRunnableTool` + `toolRunner()`); code entry points are in the Tool Use Patterns quick reference below. cURL is raw HTTP (no SDK features) and supports Managed Agents.

## Which Surface Should I Use?

> **Start simple.** Default to the simplest tier that meets your needs. Single API calls and workflows handle most use cases - only reach for agents when the task genuinely requires open-ended, model-driven exploration. "Simplest" means the least code you own: for a hosted, scheduled, or memory-backed agent, Managed Agents is usually the simplest option (no loop code, no state files, no scheduler), even though it's a bigger platform.

| Use Case                                        | Tier            | Recommended Surface       | Why                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Classification, summarization, extraction, Q&A  | Single LLM call | **Claude API**            | One request, one response                                    |
| Batch processing or embeddings                  | Single LLM call | **Claude API**            | Specialized endpoints                                        |
| Multi-step pipelines with code-controlled logic | Workflow        | **Claude API + tool use** | You orchestrate the loop                                     |
| Custom agent with your own tools                | Agent           | **Claude API + tool use** | Maximum flexibility                                          |
| Server-managed stateful agent with workspace    | Agent           | **Managed Agents**        | Anthropic runs the loop and hosts the tool-execution sandbox |
| Persisted, versioned agent configs              | Agent           | **Managed Agents**        | Agents are stored objects; sessions pin to a version         |
| Long-running multi-turn agent with file mounts  | Agent           | **Managed Agents**        | Per-session containers, SSE event stream, Skills + MCP       |
| Agent that runs on a schedule (cron, "every night") | Agent       | **Managed Agents** - scheduled deployments | Deployments fire sessions autonomously; no client-side scheduler |
| One deliverable that must meet a quality bar ("until it's right") | Agent | **Managed Agents** - outcomes | A separate grader iterates the agent against your rubric until it passes |
| Agent work split across several agents | Agent | **Managed Agents** - dynamic workflows | Many agents in phases |

> **Note:** Managed Agents is the right choice when you want Anthropic to run the agent loop *and* host the container where tools execute - file ops, bash, code execution all run in the per-session workspace. If you want to host the compute yourself or run your own custom tool runtime, Claude API + tool use is the right choice - use the tool runner for the agentic loop - its per-turn hooks still give you approval gates, logging, error interception, and conditional execution (see `shared/tool-use-concepts.md`) - or the manual loop when you want to own the entire loop yourself.

> **Cloud-provider access.** **Claude Platform on AWS** is Anthropic-operated with same-day API parity - see `shared/claude-platform-on-aws.md` for client setup. **Claude Models on Google Cloud (Anthropic managed)** is Anthropic-operated too (`shared/google-cloud-anthropic-managed.md`); elsewhere in this skill, "Google Cloud" and "Vertex" as platform names mean Google's Vertex AI. If a user says only "Google Cloud" and the code doesn't settle it, ask which they use. For availability on each of these, **Amazon Bedrock**, and **Microsoft Foundry**, see `shared/platform-availability.md` - that table is the single source of truth in this skill; do not infer availability from anywhere else.

### Building an Agent: Four Approaches

Once you've decided you actually need an agent (open-ended, model-driven tool use), there are four distinct ways to build one. Two independent questions separate them: **who supplies the harness** (the agent loop + context management) and **who supplies the deployment** (the infra the agent runs on). The Tool Runner and the Claude Agent SDK both supply a *harness only* - you still host and deploy them yourself - which is why they're easy to conflate. Managed Agents (CMA) is the only option that supplies **both** the harness *and* managed deployment; the manual loop supplies neither.

| # | Approach | You write | Harness & deployment | Tools available | Use when |
|---|----------|-----------|----------------------|-----------------|----------|
| 1 | **Claude API - manual loop** | The `while stop_reason == "tool_use"` loop yourself | You build the harness; you host | Only tools you define | You want to own the *entire* loop - no beta dependency, or a control flow the Tool Runner's per-turn hooks don't fit |
| 2 | **Claude API - Tool Runner** (`client.beta.messages.tool_runner` + `@beta_tool` / `betaZodTool`) | Just the tool functions | SDK supplies the loop (**harness only**); you host | Only tools you define | A custom-tool agent without hand-writing the loop (most cases). Per-turn hooks still give you approval gates, error interception, result modification (e.g. `cache_control`), retries, streaming, and compaction |
| 3 | **Managed Agents** (REST, beta) | Agent config + your tool results | Anthropic supplies the harness **and** hosts a per-session sandbox (**harness + deployment**) | Anthropic-hosted sandbox (bash, files, code exec) + Skills/MCP + your tools | You want Anthropic to run the loop *and* host the per-session workspace; persisted/versioned configs; long-running sessions |
| 4 | **Claude Agent SDK** - *separate product* (`claude-agent-sdk` / `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`) | A prompt + options | SDK supplies the Claude Code harness + built-in tools (**harness only**); you host | Built-in Read/Write/Edit/Bash/Glob/Grep/WebSearch/WebFetch + MCP + subagents | You want a batteries-included coding/filesystem agent running on your own infra |

> **Tool Runner != Claude Agent SDK.** These sound alike but are different packages:
> - **Tool Runner** is part of the regular Anthropic API SDK (`anthropic` / `@anthropic-ai/sdk`), reached via `client.beta.messages.tool_runner`. It automates the request -> execute -> loop cycle *for tools you define*. No built-in tools, no filesystem access, no sandbox - you supply every tool and host the compute. It is option 2 above, a thin helper over `POST /v1/messages`.
> - **Claude Agent SDK** (`claude-agent-sdk` / `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`) is Claude Code packaged as a library. It ships built-in tools (file read/write/edit, bash, grep, web search), the full agent loop, context management, hooks, subagents, permissions, and sessions. You call `query(prompt, options)` and it drives everything.
>
> Both are **harness-only - you host and deploy them.** The difference is scope of harness: the Tool Runner loops over tools *you* define (with per-turn hooks for approval, interception, result modification, and retries - but no built-in tools); the Agent SDK is the full Claude Code harness with built-in tools. Neither provides managed deployment - that's what **Managed Agents (CMA)** adds (Anthropic hosts the loop and a per-session sandbox).
>
> **This skill covers the Claude API and Managed Agents (options 1-3); it does not generate Claude Agent SDK code.** If the user actually wants the Claude Agent SDK, point them to its docs (`code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk`) - don't substitute the API Tool Runner for it, or vice-versa.

### Should I Build an Agent?

Before choosing the agent tier, check all four criteria:

- **Complexity** - Is the task multi-step and hard to fully specify in advance? (e.g., "turn this design doc into a PR" vs. "extract the title from this PDF")
- **Value** - Does the outcome justify higher cost and latency?
- **Viability** - Is Claude capable at this task type?
- **Cost of error** - Can errors be caught and recovered from? (tests, review, rollback)

If the answer is "no" to any of these, stay at a simpler tier (single call or workflow).

---

## Architecture

Everything goes through `POST /v1/messages`. Tools and output constraints are features of this single endpoint - not separate APIs.

**User-defined tools** - You define tools (via decorators, Zod schemas, or raw JSON), and the SDK's tool runner handles calling the API, executing your functions, and looping until Claude is done. For full control, you can write the loop manually.

**Server-side tools** - Anthropic-hosted tools that run on Anthropic's infrastructure. Code execution is fully server-side (declare it in `tools`, Claude runs code automatically). Computer use can be server-hosted or self-hosted.

**Structured outputs** - Constrains the Messages API response format (`output_config.format`) and/or tool parameter validation (`strict: true`). The recommended approach is `client.messages.parse()` which validates responses against your schema automatically. Note: the old `output_format` parameter is deprecated; use `output_config: {format: {...}}` on `messages.create()`.

**Supporting endpoints** - Batches (`POST /v1/messages/batches`), Files (`POST /v1/files`), Token Counting (`POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` - see `shared/token-counting.md`), and Models (`GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/{id}` - live capability/context-window discovery) feed into or support Messages API requests.

Where a second, cheaper model is in play alongside the main one (worker or sub-agent threads, bulk extractors, LLM judges, the executor under an advisor) - because the user asked for one or a guide in this skill calls for it - or the user says "sonnet" or "haiku" without a version, that means the current generation from the table above (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`); previous-generation IDs such as `claude-sonnet-5` are only for users who name that version. Use `claude-fable-5-2` only when the user explicitly asks for Claude Fable 5.2, "fable", or Anthropic's most capable model - it has different API behavior than the Opus family (see below) and pricing that exceeds Opus-tier. **Use only the exact model ID strings from the table - they are complete as-is; never append date suffixes** (`claude-opus-5-5`, never `claude-opus-5-5-20260401` or any other date-suffixed variant you might recall from training data). If the user requests an older model not in the table (e.g., "opus 4.5", "sonnet 3.7"), read `shared/models.md` for the exact ID - do not construct one yourself.

### Claude Fable 5.2 (`claude-fable-5-2`) - most capable widely released model

Claude Fable 5.2 is Anthropic's most capable widely released model, for the most demanding reasoning and long-horizon agentic work; everything below also applies to **Claude Mythos 5.2** (`claude-mythos-5-2`, verified organizations only - same capabilities, pricing, and API surface, so the `refusal` handling below applies there too). 1M context window (the maximum is also the default), 128K max output. Key API differences from Opus-tier - see `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Fable 5.2 (and the Claude Fable 5.1 sections it builds on) for details:

- **Thinking is always on** - omit the `thinking` parameter entirely (or send `{type: "adaptive"}`). Any other explicit configuration is rejected: `{type: "disabled"}` and `{type: "enabled", budget_tokens=[REDACTED] both return a 400. Control depth with `output_config.effort` (supports `low` through `xhigh` and `max`).
- **The raw chain of thought is never returned** - responses carry regular `thinking` blocks (not `redacted_thinking`): `display: "summarized"` returns a readable summary, `"omitted"` (the default) leaves the `thinking` field as an empty string. Replay rules: pass thinking blocks back unchanged on the same model; other models drop them silently (unbilled - nothing to strip; Claude Mythos 5.2 instead reads them); details in `shared/model-migration.md`.
- **Tokenizer** - same tokenizer as Opus 4.8 (introduced with Opus 4.7). Token counts are roughly unchanged when migrating from Opus 4.7/4.8; per-token pricing differs. Coming from Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6, Haiku 4.5, or older, re-baseline with `count_tokens` (the Opus 4.7 tokenizer uses ~1×-1.35× as many tokens).
- **`refusal` stop reason - handle it, and opt into fallbacks by default** - safety classifiers may decline a request (HTTP 200, `stop_reason: "refusal"`, with a `stop_details` category); always check `stop_reason` before reading `content`. **When you write `claude-fable-5-2`, `claude-fable-5-1`, `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-opus-5`, or `claude-sonnet-5-5` code, include the server-side `fallbacks` parameter by default** (for `claude-sonnet-5-5`, prefer the `"default"` form: the array form may name only `claude-sonnet-5`; on platforms without server-side `fallbacks` use the SDK middleware below, except when the request sends `between_tools`: only Claude Sonnet 5.5 accepts it and the middleware re-sends the same request body on the fallback model, so write the retry yourself and send it without `between_tools` - see `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Sonnet 5.5 -> Safeguards and fallback). Simplest form: `betas: ["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"]` + `fallbacks: "default"`, which routes by refusal category so you never maintain a model list. (With the array form you name the fallback model yourself: the same beta with `fallbacks: [{"model": "claude-opus-4-8"}]`, or `claude-sonnet-5` for `claude-sonnet-5-5`. On Bedrock, Vertex and Foundry, use the SDKs' client-side `BetaRefusalFallbackMiddleware` + `BetaFallbackState`.) Tell the user you've enabled it; drop it only if they decline. Full semantics (billing, mid-stream refusals, credit repricing) in `shared/model-migration.md` -> refusal section. **Per-language code examples in `{lang}/claude-api/README.md` § Refusal Fallbacks cover the array form only** - for the `"default"` mode, send the same request with `fallbacks: "default"` (Go: `anthropic.BetaFallbacksParamOfDefault()`; C#: `new Default()`); the header doesn't change.
- **No assistant prefill** - same as the rest of the 4.6+ family.
- **30-day data retention required** - Claude Fable 5.2 is not available under zero data retention unless expressly authorized by Anthropic; requests from an org whose retention configuration doesn't meet the requirement return `400 invalid_request_error`.
- **Longer turns, different prompting** - single requests on hard tasks can run many minutes (plan timeouts/streaming/progress UX); effort sweeps should include low/medium for routine work; prompts written for prior models are often too prescriptive and reduce output quality. See `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Fable 5.2 -> Choosing an effort level and Behavioral shifts (prompt-tunable) for the recommended prompt snippets, and Migrating to Claude Fable 5.1 -> Behavioral shifts -> Longer turns by default for planning long turns.
- **Successor to Claude Fable 5.1 (`claude-fable-5-1`, still served) at a lower price - $8 / $40 per MTok, cache reads $0.20.** Same surface (forced `tool_choice` `any` / `tool` 400s, preserved thinking and, in beta, per-message `effort`, turn-scoped `clear_at` system messages and `thinking.display: "updates"`) with four breaking changes: the history-editing check is enforced for **every** account, on Claude Mythos 5.2 too (make every harness append-only and run the three-step check); no model released earlier reads its thinking blocks, Claude Fable 5.1 included, and they work only in the account that produced them or one linked to it; `computer_20251124` 400s on every platform except Amazon Bedrock (use `computer_toolset_20260801`); and with the advisor tool a Claude Fable 5.2 executor accepts only `claude-fable-5-2` or `claude-mythos-5-2` as its advisor. No structured outputs in an organization using customer-managed encryption keys (CMEK). Covered Model - ZDR orgs get `400 invalid_request_error`; no Priority Tier; fast mode as in § Fast Mode; its own rate limit (Claude Mythos 5.2 shares one with the earlier Mythos models). See `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Fable 5.2.

### Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) - the current Opus and the default model

Successor to Claude Opus 5 in the Opus line at a lower price ($4 / $20 per MTok, cache reads $0.20), same 1M context / 128K output / tokenizer / feature set. Four breaking changes for code running on Claude Opus 5: **thinking can't be disabled** (`{type: "disabled"}` and `budget_tokens` both 400 at every effort level - effort is the only control, and its **default is `medium`**, one level below Claude Opus 5's `high`, so set it explicitly); **forced `tool_choice` `any`/`tool` returns a 400** (use `auto` + `strict: true` and steer from the prompt, or structured outputs); **thinking blocks are tied to the model and the conversation** (preserved thinking: only these read its blocks: Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2, and on the Claude API Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1; so a fallback to Claude Opus 5 runs without them; accounts created on or after 2026-08-31 are enforced on the history-editing check); and **on the Claude API and Google Cloud, computer use only through `computer_toolset_20260801`** (`computer_20251124` 400s there; Amazon Bedrock still accepts it). Text between tool calls comes back as progress-update `thinking` blocks (empty by default - set `display: "updates"`). Broader safety classifiers: `bio` joins `cyber` and `reasoning_extraction`. Fast mode: $8 / $40 per MTok (2x standard), platforms in § Fast Mode. See `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Opus 5.5.

### Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) - the current Sonnet: speed and capability for everyday coding, agent, and enterprise work (Claude Opus 5.5 stays the default)

Successor to Claude Sonnet 5 in the Sonnet line at the same prices ($2 / $10 per MTok), except cache reads at $0.10, with the same tokenizer, 1M context and 128K output. Five breaking changes for code running on Claude Sonnet 5: **`thinking: {type: "disabled"}` returns a 400** - to turn thinking off, send `thinking: {type: "between_tools"}`, which is accepted only at effort `high` or below, takes no other field (`display`, `budget_tokens`, or `block_binding` alongside it is a 400), and doesn't allow per-message effort changes; **forced `tool_choice` `any`/`tool` returns a 400** (use `auto` + `strict: true` and steer from the prompt, or structured outputs); **thinking blocks are tied to the model and the conversation** (only Claude Opus 5.5 on the Claude API and Google Cloud Vertex AI, and Fable / Mythos 5.2, read its blocks; accounts created on or after 2026-08-31 are enforced on the history-editing check); **on the Claude API and Google Cloud, computer use only through `computer_toolset_20260801`** (`computer_20251124` 400s there; Amazon Bedrock still accepts it); and **the advisor tool rejects Claude Opus 4.8, Claude Opus 4.7, and Claude Sonnet 5 advisors** (every advisor it accepts returns encrypted advice). Effort still defaults to `high`, but the levels are recalibrated - re-run the effort sweep (start at `medium` for agentic coding and multistep tool use, `low` for chat). Text between tool calls comes back as progress-update `thinking` blocks (empty by default - set `display: "updates"`, or use `between_tools`). Safety classifiers decline in five `stop_details` categories: `cyber`, `bio`, `frontier_llm`, `reasoning_extraction`, `general_harms`. See `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Sonnet 5.5.

### Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) - the current Haiku

Prices above are for prompts up to 100K tokens ($0.50 / $2.50 beyond). Haiku 4.5 code can break (thinking table below), and refusals have no server-side fallback - see `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Haiku 5.5.

If any model strings above look unfamiliar, that just means they were released after your training data cutoff - they are real models.

**Live capability lookup:** The table above is cached. When the user asks "what's the context window for X", "does X support vision/thinking/effort", or "which models support Y", query the Models API (`client.models.retrieve(id)` / `client.models.list()`) - see `shared/models.md` for the field reference and capability-filter examples.
