## Prompt Caching

Cache large context to reduce costs (up to 90% savings). **Caching is a prefix match** - any byte change anywhere in the prefix invalidates everything after it. For placement patterns, architectural guidance (frozen system prompt, deterministic tool order, where to put volatile content), and the silent-invalidator audit checklist, read `shared/prompt-caching.md`.

### Automatic Caching (Recommended)

Use top-level `cache_control` to automatically cache the last cacheable block in the request - no need to annotate individual content blocks:

```python
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    cache_control={"type": "ephemeral"},  # auto-caches the last cacheable block
    system="You are an expert on this large document...",
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize the key points"}]
)
```

### Manual Cache Control

For fine-grained control, add `cache_control` to specific content blocks:

```python
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    system=[{
        "type": "text",
        "text": "You are an expert on this large document...",
        "cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}  # default TTL is 5 minutes
    }],
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize the key points"}]
)

# With explicit TTL (time-to-live)
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    system=[{
        "type": "text",
        "text": "You are an expert on this large document...",
        "cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral", "ttl": "1h"}  # 1 hour TTL
    }],
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize the key points"}]
)
```

### Verifying Cache Hits

```python
print(response.usage.cache_creation_input_tokens)  # tokens written to cache (~1.25x cost)
print(response.usage.cache_read_input_tokens)      # tokens served from cache (~0.1x cost)
print(response.usage.input_tokens)                 # uncached tokens (full cost)
```

If `cache_read_input_tokens` is zero across repeated identical-prefix requests, a silent invalidator is at work - `datetime.now()` or a UUID in the system prompt, unsorted `json.dumps()`, or a varying tool set. See `shared/prompt-caching.md` for the full audit table.

---

## Extended Thinking

> **Fable / Mythos 5/5.1/5.2, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, and Sonnet 4.6:** Use adaptive thinking. `budget_tokens` is removed on Fable / Mythos 5/5.1/5.2, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, and 4.7 (400 if sent); deprecated on Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6.
> **Claude Opus 5.5:** thinking is always on - omit `thinking` (or send `{"type": "adaptive"}`, which is equivalent); `{"type": "disabled"}` returns a 400 at every effort, as does a thinking budget. Control depth with `output_config.effort` instead - the default is `medium` on this model, where Claude Opus 5 defaults to `high`.
> **Claude Opus 5:** thinking is on by default - omitting `thinking` runs adaptive (`{"type": "adaptive"}` is equivalent), unlike Opus 4.8/4.7 where omitting it meant no thinking. `{"type": "disabled"}` is accepted only at effort `high` or lower; pairing it with `xhigh`/`max` returns a 400.
> **Claude Haiku 5.5:** adaptive thinking only, on by default; `budget_tokens` returns a 400.
> **Older models:** Use `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens=[REDACTED] (must be < `max_tokens`, min 1024).

```python
# Fable / Mythos 5/5.1/5.2 / Claude Opus 5.5 / Claude Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7 / 4.6: adaptive thinking (recommended)
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    thinking={"type": "adaptive", "display": "summarized"},  # display opt-in: default is omitted (empty thinking text) on Fable 5/5.1/5.2, Mythos 5/5.1/5.2, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8/4.7, Claude Sonnet 5.5, and Claude Sonnet 5
    output_config={"effort": "high"},  # low | medium | high | xhigh | max
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Solve this step by step..."}]
)

# Access thinking and response
for block in response.content:
    if block.type == "thinking":
        print(f"Thinking: {block.thinking}")
    elif block.type == "text":
        print(f"Response: {block.text}")
```

---

## Error Handling

```python
import anthropic

try:
    response = client.messages.create(...)
except anthropic.BadRequestError as e:
    print(f"Bad request: {e.message}")
except anthropic.AuthenticationError:
    print("Invalid API key")
except anthropic.PermissionDeniedError:
    print("API key lacks required permissions")
except anthropic.NotFoundError:
    print("Invalid model or endpoint")
except anthropic.RateLimitError as e:
    retry_after = int(e.response.headers.get("retry-after", "60"))
    print(f"Rate limited. Retry after {retry_after}s.")
except anthropic.APIStatusError as e:
    if e.status_code >= 500:
        print(f"Server error ({e.status_code}). Retry later.")
    else:
        print(f"API error: {e.message}")
except anthropic.APIConnectionError:
    print("Network error. Check internet connection.")
```

---

## Response Helpers

Every response object exposes `_request_id` (populated from the `request-id` header) - log it when reporting failures to Anthropic. Despite the underscore prefix, this property is public.

```python
message = client.messages.create(...)
print(message._request_id)       # req_018EeWyXxfu5pfWkrYcMdjWG
print(message.to_json())          # serialize the Pydantic model
print(message.to_dict())          # plain dict
```

To access raw headers or other response metadata, use `.with_raw_response`:

```python
raw = client.messages.with_raw_response.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello"}],
)
print(raw.headers.get("request-id"))
message = raw.parse()  # the Message object messages.create() would have returned
```

## Multi-Turn Conversations

The API is stateless - send the full conversation history each time (unless you use a message thread: `shared/message-threads.md`).

```python
class ConversationManager:
    """Manage multi-turn conversations with the Claude API."""

    def __init__(self, client: anthropic.Anthropic, model: str, system: str = None):
        self.client = client
        self.model = model
        self.system = system
        self.messages = []

    def send(self, user_message: str, **kwargs) -> str:
        """Send a message and get a response."""
        self.messages.append({"role": "user", "content": user_message})

        response = self.client.messages.create(
            model=self.model,
            max_tokens=[REDACTED] 16000),
            system=self.system,
            messages=self.messages,
            **kwargs
        )

        assistant_message = next(
            (b.text for b in response.content if b.type == "text"), ""
        )
        self.messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": assistant_message})

        return assistant_message

# Usage
conversation = ConversationManager(
    client=anthropic.Anthropic(),
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    system="You are a helpful assistant."
)

response1 = conversation.send("My name is Alice.")
response2 = conversation.send("What's my name?")  # Claude remembers "Alice"
```

**Rules:**

- Consecutive same-role messages are allowed - the API combines them into a single turn
- First message must be `user`
- `role: "system"` messages are allowed mid-conversation on supporting models (no beta header needed) - see § Mid-conversation system messages above

---

### Compaction (long conversations)

> **Beta, Fable / Mythos 5/5.1/5.2, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, and Sonnet 4.6.** When input tokens reach the compaction trigger (default 150K), compaction automatically summarizes earlier context server-side. The API returns a `compaction` block; you must pass it back on subsequent requests - append `response.content`, not just the text.

```python
import anthropic

client = anthropic.Anthropic()
messages = []

def chat(user_message: str) -> str:
    messages.append({"role": "user", "content": user_message})

    response = client.beta.messages.create(
        betas=["compact-2026-01-12"],
        model="claude-opus-5-5",
        max_tokens=[REDACTED]
        messages=messages,
        context_management={
            "edits": [{"type": "compact_20260112"}]
        }
    )

    # Append full content - compaction blocks must be preserved
    messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": response.content})

    return next(block.text for block in response.content if block.type == "text")

# Compaction triggers automatically when context grows large
print(chat("Help me build a Python web scraper"))
print(chat("Add support for JavaScript-rendered pages"))
print(chat("Now add rate limiting and error handling"))
```

---

## Stop Reasons

The `stop_reason` field in the response indicates why the model stopped generating:

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `end_turn` | Claude finished its response naturally |
| `max_tokens` | Hit the `max_tokens` limit - increase it or use streaming |
| `stop_sequence` | Hit a custom stop sequence |
| `tool_use` | Claude wants to call a tool - execute it and continue |
| `pause_turn` | Model paused and can be resumed (agentic flows) |
| `refusal` | Claude refused for safety reasons - check `stop_details` |

### Structured Stop Details

When `stop_reason` is `"refusal"`, the response includes a `stop_details` object with structured information about the refusal:

```python
if response.stop_reason == "refusal" and response.stop_details:
    print(f"Category: {response.stop_details.category}")   # e.g. "cyber", "bio", "reasoning_extraction", "frontier_llm", or None - see docs for the full set
    print(f"Explanation: {response.stop_details.explanation}")
```

### Refusal Fallbacks (Claude Fable 5.2) - opt in by default

Fallbacks are **opt-in**: without them a refused request simply stops. Include the server-side `fallbacks` parameter in `claude-fable-5-2` code by default - on a policy decline the API re-runs the same request on the fallback model inside the same call. A mid-stream decline is billed at normal rates, and the rescue bills at the fallback model's own rates, with cache repricing applied automatically; for a decline before any output, see [How refusals are billed](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/refusals-and-fallback#how-refusals-are-billed).

```python
response = client.beta.messages.create(
    model="claude-fable-5-2",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    betas=["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"],
    fallbacks=[{"model": "claude-opus-4-8"}],
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "..."}],
)

# Switch points: one fallback block per model that ran and declined this turn
for block in response.content:
    if block.type == "fallback":
        print(f"{block.from_.model} declined; {block.to.model} continued")

# Served-by signal - covers sticky turns, which carry no fallback block.
# Pair with stop_reason: the fallback model can itself refuse.
fallback_ran = any(
    entry.type == "fallback_message" for entry in response.usage.iterations or []
)
if fallback_ran and response.stop_reason != "refusal":
    print(f"Served by {response.model}")
```

A `stop_reason: "refusal"` on the final response means the whole chain refused. This array form takes `server-side-fallback-2026-06-01` or `-2026-07-01`; the `fallbacks: "default"` scalar form needs `-2026-07-01` (see `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Opus 5 -> New API features). Any other `server-side-fallback-*` value returns a 400. The parameter is rejected on the Batches API and unavailable on Amazon Bedrock, Vertex AI, and Microsoft Foundry - register the client-side `BetaRefusalFallbackMiddleware` on the client there instead. Full semantics (sticky routing, billing, streaming, echoing fallback turns back): `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Fable 5.1 -> `refusal` stop reason.

## Cost Optimization Strategies

### 1. Use Prompt Caching for Repeated Context

```python
# Automatic caching (simplest - caches the last cacheable block)
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    cache_control={"type": "ephemeral"},
    system=large_document_text,  # e.g., 50KB of context
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize the key points"}]
)

# First request: full cost
# Subsequent requests: ~90% cheaper for cached portion
```

### 2. Choose the Right Model

```python
# Default to Opus for most tasks
response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",  # $4.00/$20.00 per 1M tokens
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Explain quantum computing"}]
)

# Use Sonnet for high-volume production workloads
standard_response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-sonnet-5-5",  # $2.00/$10.00 per 1M tokens
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Summarize this document"}]
)

# Use Haiku only for simple, speed-critical tasks
simple_response = client.messages.create(
    model="claude-haiku-5-5",  # $0.10/$0.50 per 1M tokens
    max_tokens=[REDACTED]
    output_config={"effort": "low"},
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Classify this as positive or negative"}]
)
```

### 3. Use Token Counting Before Requests

```python
count_response = client.messages.count_tokens(
    model="claude-opus-5-5",
    messages=messages,
    system=system
)

estimated_input_cost = count_response.input_tokens * 0.000004  # $4/1M tokens
print(f"Estimated input cost: ${estimated_input_cost:.4f}")
```

## Retry with Exponential Backoff

> **Note:** The Anthropic SDK automatically retries rate limit (429) and server errors (5xx) with exponential backoff. You can configure this with `max_retries` (default: 2). Only implement custom retry logic if you need behavior beyond what the SDK provides.

```python
import time
import random
import anthropic

def call_with_retry(
    client: anthropic.Anthropic,
    max_retries: int = 5,
    base_delay: float = 1.0,
    max_delay: float = 60.0,
    **kwargs
):
    """Call the API with exponential backoff retry."""
    last_exception = None

    for attempt in range(max_retries):
        try:
            return client.messages.create(**kwargs)
        except anthropic.RateLimitError as e:
            last_exception = e
        except anthropic.APIStatusError as e:
            if e.status_code >= 500:
                last_exception = e
            else:
                raise  # Client errors (4xx except 429) should not be retried

        delay = min(base_delay * (2 ** attempt) + random.uniform(0, 1), max_delay)
        print(f"Retry {attempt + 1}/{max_retries} after {delay:.1f}s")
        time.sleep(delay)

    raise last_exception
```

   - Hard rules (CLAUDE.md): never read/print/commit `.env` or keys; only `mde_llm_gateway` imports a vendor SDK; live mode is the single `LLM_MODE=live` switch (never add a [live-mode rule phrase]/flag/gate); every LLM call goes through the gateway with a call spec, mock handler and fallback; money is Decimal/int micros; log decisions in `docs/implementation/DECISIONS.md` (next number D-171) and never ask the user questions (except the overhaul approval); commit checkpoints (commit pending work, tag `pre/<task>-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>`, new branch for overhauls); `uv.lock` is never committed from a worktree; commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <[EMAIL]>`; PRs end with "🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)". Tests are offline except `live` markers.
   - Gateway architecture: `Provider` protocol (`name`, `mode`, `billable`, `async complete(ProviderRequest) -> ProviderResponse`); the provider makes exactly ONE call, and the gateway owns retries, repair, budget and metering. Factory `_build_provider` picks a provider by `LLM_MODE` (mock/replay/live; live → `OpenAIResponsesProvider`, optionally wrapped in `RecordingProvider`), `openai.AsyncOpenAI(max_retries=0)`. Budget guard with `HARD_CAP_USD = 8.00` on the shared ledger. The Research app has its own period ledger/scope (cap auto-raised to total/0.8).
   - Settings: `openai_api_key`, `openai_model`, `llm_mode`, `model_routes` dict, `model_for_route()` (falls back to `openai_model`), `secret_values()`, `describe_presence`. `gating.require_api_key` reads only the OpenAI key.
   - Pricing: `pricing.yaml` per model (input, cached_input, output per million; optional long_context threshold with multipliers applied to the whole request); unknown models are refused.
   - Anthropic API facts (claude-api skill, cached 2026-11-03): `claude-haiku-5-5` $0.10/$0.50 per M tokens for prompts ≤100K ($0.50/$2.50 above, whole request; cache write 0.125/0.20, read 0.01); `claude-sonnet-5-5` $2/$10 (cache write 2.50/4, read 0.10); `claude-opus-5-5` $4/$20 (write 5/8, read 0.20); `claude-fable-5-2` $8/$40. Haiku 5.5 has adaptive thinking on by default (disabled accepted only at effort ≤ high), effort low–max with default medium, no `budget_tokens`, non-default sampling params return 400, no assistant prefill. Forced `tool_choice` any/tool returns 400 on Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5.5. Structured outputs use `output_config: {format: {type: "json_schema", schema}}`. Unsupported schema keywords: numerical constraints, minLength/maxLength, complex array constraints, recursion, additionalProperties ≠ false. Possible `stop_reason`: `refusal`, `max_tokens`. Usage `input_tokens` excludes `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` (differs from OpenAI). Errors: 400, 401, 402 billing, 403, 404, 413, 429 (retry-after), 500, 529 overloaded. The Python SDK: `anthropic.AsyncAnthropic`, `messages.create`; the skill requires the official SDK in Python projects (no raw httpx); anthropic 1.x uses `httpx2`.
   - Research agent specifics: routes `fast` (extraction writer), `standard` (checker), `deep` (assemble); call specs with `reasoning_effort` (low), `max_output_tokens`, `repair`; the checker is a second model call with batch size 15; `checker_vendor: same` detected by comparing model ids; the budget period restart via the Budget page; the app is started with `RESEARCH_LIVE_BUDGET_USD` / `RESEARCH_TRIPWIRE_USD` env; Tavily default search (`.runtime/settings.json` = tavily), Serper second engine; eval CLI `uv run python -m mde_research_agent.eval planted|probe|reextract`; run folders in `agents/research/.runtime/runs/` (git-ignored); the app API at `http://127.0.0.1:8120` with headers `Authorization: Bearer dev-token`, `X-Tenant-Id: demo`, `X-User-Id: dev`.

Progress: the vendor-independence work is committed (rule 14, Anthropic provider, per-call routing, Research wiring; about 2,500 offline tests pass). The live checks on Claude are done. The Small yoga run is now in progress, and I'm waiting for it to finish before writing up its numbers.

### CLAUDE

The work is done and I'm on the last step. The full unit suite is at 86% and passing so far. Once it ends I'll commit the report and saved outputs, tag and push.

- **Vendor independence:** CLAUDE.md rule 14 is in. A second provider (Claude) and a router are in the gateway, and switching vendor is one line, `MODEL_ROUTES`. It is not yet true for the whole repo: Compass still names the OpenAI model.
- **Small run (yoga):** the final Claude run filled 19 of 19 required slots with 11% of facts unverified, against 15 of 19 on the earlier OpenAI run.
- **Medium and Big:** Medium completed (78% of required slots). Big finished partial (90%, 6 required slots empty).
- **Consumer probe:** it did not pass. Research helped the stand-in Spec, but "invented" statements rose from 0 to 23, mostly on features and screens where the research was thin.
- **Money:** about USD 3.0 of model calls against your USD 5 cap. Counting search credits at the highest rate, USD 4.66 of the 4.95 period is booked.
- **Saved outputs:** each run's `research.json`, readable report and numbers are in `docs/research-agent/outputs/claude-2026-11-13/`. The full report with my opinion is `docs/research-agent/claude-provider-test-report-2026-11-13.md`.

Two things to know:

- Haiku 5.5 only works properly with its lowest thinking level raised to medium (`effort_floor` on its price row).
- `just typecheck` fails repo-wide with 496 errors. None are in the 23 files this branch changed (0 errors there); they come from Mac vs Windows and missing playwright.

I did not run Dakar, Pune, the hospital input or Central America, or Medium and Big on Tavily; the cap ruled them out. The Research budget period is at its tripwire, so restart it on the Budget page before the next run.

Report for`

### CLAUDE

The Research agent now runs on Claude, and switching vendors is one setting in the gateway. On every input I ran, the Claude runs met the 70% required-slot bar. The consumer probe is the exception: it did not pass its own bar (details below).

The full report with my opinion is `docs/research-agent/claude-provider-test-report-2026-11-13.md`. Each run's output is saved in `docs/research-agent/outputs/claude-2026-11-13/` (the `research.json`, a readable report and a numbers file per run). The branch `feat/llm-providers` is pushed with tags `pre/llm-providers-20261113-2128` and `gateway/llm-providers-20261113`. The unit suite passes (10,607) and the layering checks are green; `just ci-fast` still stops at repo-wide pyright (see the last bullet).

- **Model independence:** `CLAUDE.md` rule 14 now says no agent or engine may depend on a model vendor, and a test keeps vendor and model names out of the Research source. To run on Claude, set `MODEL_ROUTES=claude-haiku-5-5` in the launch environment; to go back to OpenAI, `MODEL_ROUTES=gpt-6-luna`. Never put these in `.env`.
- **What Claude needed to work:**
  - **Schema limits:** the first run lost 39 of 150 extraction calls because Claude refuses schemas with more than 16 nullable fields or an oversized grammar. I fixed it inside the gateway.
  - **Thinking level:** Haiku 5.5 filled in details the pages don't state until I raised its lowest thinking level to medium. That is a setting on its price row, so no engine changed.
- **Results on Claude Haiku 5.5:**

| Run | Required slots | Unverified facts | USD |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Small, yoga (Tavily) | 19 of 19 | 11.5% | 0.61 |

| Big, freight (Serper) | 57 of 63 (90%), partial | 16% | 1.03 |

  The earlier OpenAI run on the same Small input had 15 of 19 slots. Each is a single run on a moving web, so I wouldn't read it as "Claude beats OpenAI".
- **Sonnet 5.5 checker:** it failed the planted-error test (85% caught, against a 90% bar) at 17 times the cost, so all routes use Haiku.
- **Consumer probe (Medium):** it did not pass. Research cut the stand-in Spec's "assumed" statements from 59 to 24, but "invented" rose from 0 to 23, mostly on features and screens where the research was thin. My opinion is that the Spec engine must treat any statement without a Research id as an assumption, in code.
- **Money:** about USD 3.0 of model calls against your USD 5 cap, plus USD 1.69 of search credits booked at the highest rate. The period shows USD 4.66 of 4.95.
- **Not run:** Dakar, Pune, hospital, Central America, and Medium and Big on Tavily. The cap ruled them out, so Medium and Big ran on Serper, and Tavily is restored as the saved default. The Research budget is at its tripwire, so restart it on the Budget page before any new run.
- **Known gaps:**
  - **Compass:** it still names the OpenAI model, so rule 14 does not yet hold repo-wide.
  - **Pyright:** the repo-wide check has 496 errors, none in the 23 files this branch changed. They come from Mac vs Windows and a missing playwright.

When OpenAI credits return, the most useful next test is a cross-vendor pair (Claude extracts, OpenAI checks). Entity swaps are the weakest spot even after the fix (89.8% caught).


### USER

So what do you think about our agent, research agent works? Be blunt and honest and go ahead. Tell me short and honest.


### CLAUDE

It works as a pipeline, but I haven't proven it's trustworthy enough for Spec to build on.

**What's solid**
- Every run finished, no fact reaches the output without a quote that code checked against the page, and the output file is valid.
- Small and Medium met the 70% required-slot bar, and Big's 90% was only partial.
- Its main weakness is cost: search credits are most of every run.

**What I don't trust yet**
- **Weak topics:** the same ones fail in every run: comparable apps, some journeys, and screens for the big app. The consumer probe showed what that does downstream: the stand-in Spec invented 23 statements where research was thin.
- **Model sensitivity:** Haiku at the default thinking level gave 43% unverified facts and 4 of 19 slots. It only became good after I found and fixed two Claude-specific problems, and unit tests could not have found either; only live runs did. I can't promise there isn't a third.
- **Checker:** it almost never says "not supported" (under 1% on real runs). It passes the planted-error test, but real mistakes look different from planted ones.
- **Relevance:** Global vendors like Klaviyo and Braze appear for an Indian yoga studio, and Porto's payments provider has almost no facts from its own site.

**What I haven't checked**
- I read 14 facts by hand, not the 60 you'd want, and those runs are one sample each.
- I haven't compared it to OpenAI on the same day, and Medium and Big ran on Serper, not your default Tavily.

**Next:** spot-check 60 facts yourself, fix comparables and screens discovery, and make Spec refuse any statement without a Research id.
